## C159 · table r8

> Korean shortage cohort · 69; dulaglutide cessation · HbA1c 7.0% before, 8.1% afterward · Three months

- **Choe et al. 2025** · full text · supported — “The initial cohort comprised 179 individuals aged between 20 and 90 years, all diagnosed with type 2 diabetes and treated with a combination of dulaglutide and metformin. These participants had baseline HbA1c levels of 9.0% or lower (Supplemental Fig. S1). We excluded 40 individuals who were already using insulin, 44 who started insulin therapy after discontinuing GLP-1 receptor agonists, and 70 with missing baseline or follow-up HbA1c values. Following these exclusions, 69 participants were eligible for the analysis.

Twelve discontinued dulaglutide without any replacement, 27 switched to dipeptidyl peptidase-4 inhibitors (DPP4i), 26 to sodium glucose cotransporter-2 inhibitors (SGLT2i), and three to a combination of both (Table 1). HbA1c levels increased from 7.0%±0.9% to 8.1%±1.4% (P<0.001) 3 months after stopping dulaglutide (Fig. 1). Similarly, the mean fasting glucose concentration rose from 129±31 to 156±50 mg/dL (P<0.001), while C-peptide concentration decreased from 4.93±3.38 to 3.44±1.85 ng/mL (P=0.007). Discontinuation of GLP-1 receptor agonists or switching to DPP4i or SGLT2i alone resulted in worsening HbA1c levels at follow-up (by 1.5%, 1.1%, and 1.1%, respectively). Those who switched to a combination of DPP4i and SGLT2i maintained stable glucose levels, although the small sample size limits definitive conclusions. Body mass index increased in those who switched to DPP4i therapy (from 28.4± 5.9 to 28.8±5.9 kg/m2, P=0.017). An exploratory analysis of those formerly using insulin in conjunction with GLP-1 receptor agonist (who were originally excluded from the main analysis) showed an increase in HbA1c (1.2%±1.3%, P=0.006) (Supplemental Table S1), suggesting that the insulin dose adjustments made after discontinuing GLP-1 receptor agonists were insufficient to maintain glycemic control.” (Both abstract and Results report 69 analyzed dulaglutide stoppers/switchers, mean HbA1c 7.0% to 8.1% at three months; table avoids implying semaglutide.) Source exclusion tallies are internally hard to reconcile, but both abstract and Results independently identify the 69-person analysis.

## C168 · ¶39 s6

- **Yevusiak et al. 2026** · full text · supported — “There is little clinical data on the best strategy to achieve weight maintenance and cardiometabolic benefits following semaglutide discontinuation. This raises the question of whether a gradual de-escalation of semaglutide could ameliorate the tendency for weight regain and cardiometabolic deterioration. Thus, we propose a randomized controlled trial to determine whether a gradual dose reduction of semaglutide prior to complete discontinuation is associated with differential changes in weight and cardiometabolic profile as compared to immediate treatment cessation.

Materials and methods

Study design

We propose an open-label, parallel-arm, randomized controlled trial designed to evaluate the impact of semaglutide discontinuation strategies on weight and cardiometabolic outcomes. The total study duration will be 32 weeks, including a 16-week treatment discontinuation phase followed by a post-discontinuation follow-up period ( Figs 1  and  2 ).” … “Randomization and interventions

Participants will be randomized in a 1:1 ratio using a random number generator to one of two semaglutide discontinuation strategies: (i) immediate cessation, in which participants will continue their current dose for 16 weeks followed by complete discontinuation, or (ii) gradual dose reduction, in which the semaglutide dosage will be reduced by 25% every 4 weeks until complete discontinuation at week 16. All participants will receive standard lifestyle recommendations, including physical activity, portion control, and self-monitoring of weight.” … “No datasets were generated or analyzed during the current study protocol. Data will be made available upon study completion.” (Explicit data availability states no datasets generated or analyzed and reporting awaits study completion; this supports no completed outcome results in this protocol.) ## Whole-review interpretation

**preserved** — Stopping semaglutide commonly reverses much of substantial treatment-related weight loss and some metabolic gains, while some people retain loss. Direct randomized withdrawal supports a drug contribution even with counselling; studies leave individual appetite/symptom timing and an effective semaglutide exit strategy uncertain.

Scientific basis: C020, C025, C047, C054, C060, C076, C079, C083, C085, C101, C105, C108, C110, C117, C125, C137, C138, C142, C147

Read the entire revised review after all151 source-pair judgments and independently re-inspected all three actual PNGs. The central takeaway is preserved: substantial average regain and metabolic deterioration often follow cessation, but retained loss and contrary routine-care trajectories make complete regain uncertain. Named STEP1/STEP4 clauses preserve their populations; the crossover now distinguishes30 assigned from28 completing; insulin glucose dependence is confined to the supported mechanism; uncertain appetite-return timing is separated from gradual drug clearance. The HbA1c result now uses its actual four-study/219 denominator, rather than the weight endpoint five-study/853 denominator. Scope separates scientific no-results protocol content from Wang access disclosure and distinguishes literature discovery from Crossref reference verification. The planned tapering comparison remains an uncompleted question, without implying a proven exit schedule or immediate stopping at randomized baseline. Actual figures preserve their respective scientific tasks and every quantitative caption reference. No located scientific meaning failure remains. Following an export apparatus mutation, independently reread the immutable inspected copy and compared all body/table/captions/scope with current review: identical. Source receipt counts/tiers and a receipt pointer are the only added apparatus. C163/C166 scope attestations now bind that exact inspected immutable copy rather than the export-mutated file.

Limitations: STEP1 is a selected completer extension with both medication and counselling stopped; STEP4 selected target-dose tolerant completers. Meal tests assess on-treatment laboratory intake, not a personal post-stopping timetable, and gastric emptying uses an indirect proxy. Appetite-return evidence is a small uncontrolled abstract. The diabetes meta-analysis estimates highly heterogeneous before/after changes across agents/care and includes related trial cohorts; four HbA1c studies reported219 participants are not four independent replications or an Ozempic-specific randomized estimate. Routine-care trajectories use initiation time and refill gaps with unmeasured lifestyle. PCOS follow-up has no metformin counterfactual. Danish liraglutide follow-up has attrition and multiplicity limits; actual CONSORT pixels independently authenticate195 randomized. Protocol is substantive fulltext with no generated/analyzed datasets; its visit/table time-origin labels have inconsistencies, so no precise primary-visit contrast is asserted. Qualification gold, separate writer materials and other cases were not accessed. Initial audit incidental embedded writer-reading-note/evidence-assessment exposure is preserved in the initial report; not used as scientific evidence.
- Figure `ozempic-weight-course.png`: Actual teal dots at weeks0,68,120 are respectively0,-17.3,-5.6 percent from original starting weight; dashed line marks treatment end at68. Scale and positions preserve average loss then partial regain with final weight below baseline. No uncertainty bars appear; caption explicitly calls connecting segments visual guides, so intermediate linear kinetics are not represented as measured. Figure is STEP1 semaglutide average, not a 100kg individual or a comparison of drug-only withdrawal. (basis: C020, C153)
- Figure `ozempic-meal-test.png`: Actual image shows a full served plate, arrow to plate with leftovers/fork, arrow to a lined intake record, with labels Serve in excess, Eat until full, Record intake. It communicates the ad-libitum measurement procedure in temporal order. No drug-group labels, calorie/percent scale or result comparison appears; food composition and remaining fraction are schematic and cannot be read as the24% or35% results. (basis: C045, C047, C051, C053, C068)
- Figure `ozempic-continue-stop.png`: Actual teal Continue point is -7.9% with attached whisker spanning approximately-8.6 to-7.2, and rust Switch to placebo point +6.9% with whisker approximately5.8 to7.9. A dashed zero baseline says No weight change; axes Assigned treatment and Weight change after assignment (%) preserve post-run-in reference. The shared scale correctly communicates opposing treatment-policy mean directions over48weeks with95% group intervals, not total loss from initial treatment start or individual ranges. Caption retains2.4mg run-in selection and counselling in both groups. (basis: C076, C077, C090, C091, C156)

## Outcome certainty (separate from source support)

- Stopping semaglutide after substantial weight loss is commonly followed by substantial regain over the next year.: moderate — observational extension327; 17.3% loss,11.6 percentage points regained; net5.6%; SD9.3,7.7,8.9. Main boundary: 2.4mg without diabetes; drug and support both ended; selected completers.
- Continuing semaglutide maintained weight loss better than switching to placebo despite continuing lifestyle counselling in both groups.: high — randomized803 after20-week run-in; -7.9% CI-8.6 to-7.2 versus+6.9% CI5.8 to7.9; difference-14.8points CI-16.0 to-13.5 at48weeks. Main boundary: 2.4mg tolerant completers without diabetes; changes relative to randomization weight.
- Some people retained meaningful weight loss a year after semaglutide withdrawal.: moderate — 95/197=48.2% retained>=5% at120weeks. Main boundary: available final site measurements; average does not determine individual destiny.
- Semaglutide reduced appetite and food intake in controlled meal tests in adults with obesity.: moderate — crossover30,12weeks titrated1mg; total intake24% lower ETD-3036kJ CI-4209 to-1864. Main boundary: on-treatment laboratory meals, not uncontrolled daily life or withdrawal trajectory.
- A small semaglutide trial found lower lunch intake without evidence of delayed gastric emptying on its indirect test at the end of treatment.: low — 72 adults20weeks2.4mg;35% lower lunch; no delayed emptying by paracetamol test. Main boundary: proxy not gold-standard solid-emptying measurement; do not say stomach never slows.
- Semaglutide has an elimination half-life of approximately one week.: high — 17 human pharmacokinetic studies; approximately1week. Main boundary: concentration decay is not a measured timetable for return of hunger or adverse events.
- Semaglutide improves glycemic control through glucose-dependent insulin secretion and reduced glucagon secretion.: high — background mechanism. Main boundary: mechanism not a post-withdrawal clinical effect estimate.
- In type 2 diabetes, stopping GLP-1 medicines was associated with higher HbA1c in pooled withdrawal studies.: moderate — 4 HbA1c studies/219 participants; HbA1c+0.65percentage points CI0.22–1.08; heterogeneous I2=96.83%; end-treatment versus off-treatment >=12weeks. Main boundary: varied drugs and replacement care; cannot give precise Ozempic-only effect.
- A shortage-driven dulaglutide interruption was followed by worsening glycemic control within three months.: low — 69T2D;7.0%SD0.9 to8.1%SD1.4 at3months; fasting129 to156mg/dL. Main boundary: dulaglutide not Ozempic; selected noninsulin cohort with available labs; observational.
- Blood pressure and several lipid improvements deteriorated after semaglutide withdrawal in STEP 4.: moderate — STEP4secondary analysis803; deterioration20–68weeks. Main boundary: same STEP4 trial family; risk markers not measured event consequences of stopping.
- New gastrointestinal events were less common after switching to placebo than with continued semaglutide in STEP 4.: moderate — 41.9% vs26.1%;535continue268switch; randomized48weeks. Main boundary: corrected June2023 publisher abstract confirms table; not symptom-resolution times or guarantee.
- A large routine-care cohort found relatively stable weight trajectories after discontinuation, despite less overall weight loss among stoppers.: low — 7881initiators; >90day refill gap; one year from initiation; restart possible. Main boundary: mixed semaglutide/tirzepatide, doses, follow-up length and unmeasured lifestyle; confounding.
- Women with PCOS who continued metformin retained much of their semaglutide-associated weight loss over two years in a small uncontrolled study.: low — 25women;1mg16weeks; metformin2000mg/day ongoing; median101→92→95kg; aboutone-third regained. Main boundary: no non-metformin comparator; cannot credit metformin as proven prevention; selectedPCOS.
- Prior supervised exercise was associated with less post-treatment regain than liraglutide alone in a randomized trial follow-up.: low — off-treatment9.6kg liraglutide,3.6exercise,7.1combination; liraglutide vs exercise6.0kg CI2.1–10.0; vscombination2.5kg CI-1.5–6.5. Main boundary: 195randomized109attended; not semaglutide; multiplicity limits several comparisons; no universal exit recipe.
- A recent meta-analysis modelled rapid regain after newer incretin medicines but its time-to-baseline estimate extended beyond most observed follow-up.: moderate — 37studies9341; newer semaglutide/tirzepatide0.8kg/month CI0.7–0.9; projection1.5yearsCI1.0–1.9. Main boundary: few beyond1year; projection not observation; overlap central trials.
- A published semaglutide trial protocol proposes comparing gradual dose reduction with immediate cessation.: low — protocol compares16week gradual reduction versus immediate stopping; no outcome dataset. Main boundary: protocol hypotheses not results; no inference tapering works.
- Canadian adult obesity guidance recommends long-term use of effective obesity medication to avoid regain and regression of benefits.: high — 2025 guideline recommendation4. Main boundary: attributed adult obesity guidance, not individual instruction or diabetes withdrawal trial.
- Oral semaglutide reduced laboratory food intake and improved eating control in a small study of people with type 2 diabetes.: low — 15randomized13evaluable;12weeks;38.9%lower intakeETD-5096kJ CI-7000 to-3192.1. Main boundary: oral formulation, small mostlymale allwhite; not injected Ozempic withdrawal study.
- HbA1c reflects chronic blood glucose and can be compared with average glucose over the preceding three months.: high — 507adults; definition only. Main boundary: erratum about mmol/L conversion formula irrelevant to definition; do not use uncorrected formula.
- A small uncontrolled human study reported returning appetite after semaglutide withdrawal.: low — 28women;36week2.4mg then12weeks off;78.5%reported defined appetite rebound. Main boundary: abstract only; no claims about rat brain/gut mechanism or universal prevalence.
- Evidence for reducing doses or spacing injections to preserve weight loss remains limited.: low — narrative proposals with very little literature. Main boundary: no proven general schedule or individual advice.
